# Promises: the product record for guide writers

Releases reconciled: 0.3, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8, 0.9 (including alpha.1), 0.10 (including alpha.1), 0.11 (including alpha.1), 0.12 and 0.16. Status checked: 0.13–0.15 and 0.17 (no additional released section).

Reconciled 2026-11-03 for C1. The table keeps journey steps and variants, capability statements and terms as stable primary records. Stories and acceptance scenarios cite their primary record rather than repeating it. Reader names follow guide/AGENTS.md. AI and agent items are held for the later writing wave; digests and workspace following are in scope now.

Release reconciliation completed for the ordinary product record. All 1,246 retained release leads have a disposition: 904 merged into semantic records, 28 superseded, 131 dropped, and 183 AI/agent leads retained unchanged for the later wave. The per-release resolution files preserve the source, destination and reason for every disposition. This is a documentation inventory, not runtime acceptance.

Source keys: UJ = docs/product-functionality/USER_JOURNEYS.md; CF = docs/product-functionality/CURRENT_FUNCTIONALITY.md; GL = docs/product-functionality/GLOSSARY.md; US = docs/product-functionality/USER_STORIES.md; AS = docs/product-functionality/ACCEPTANCE_SCENARIOS.md; CL = CHANGELOG.md; PL = the named docs/BASE_0_N_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md. A release-scoped source such as `0.8 D8` is distinct from `0.7 D8`.

Primary records: 1095 (971 current ordinary records and 124 deferred AI/agent records). Release statements: R001–R164; C1b added R055–R164 (110). Deferred mechanical AI/agent evidence: 183 leads, outside these primary counts.

## Superseded statements (do not write as current behavior)

- US-P08, reusable model modules, is unbuilt roadmap work and is excluded from current promises. Its source and excluded coverage identity C1799 are retained.

- 0.6 WP4 per-record draft/activation/retirement: superseded by workspace change sets and publications (0.6 WP5–WP8, ADR-024/026/027; J05–J07).
- 0.5 single-process model administration restriction: superseded by 0.11 N1/N3; J12 covers several processes.
- 0.7/0.8 lower-case error spellings: superseded by 0.9 T1b; use upper-case codes.
- 0.8 D17 unsupported split topology: superseded by 0.11 N1/N3.
- 0.8 D35 mouse-only tree drag: superseded by 0.11 U1; J14.10 covers touch.
- 0.8 D41 5,000-record restructuring: superseded by 0.8 D44's 500-record limit; BEHAVIOUR-RULES B6.
- 0.9 C-D32 backslash-escaped parameter lists: superseded by C3b's repeated or comma-separated parameters with percent-encoded commas.
- 0.9 C-D96 separate webhook encryption key: superseded by 0.12's SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY; run/secrets-and-keys.
- 0.9 C-D94/C-D110 acknowledgeLoss: superseded by reset; R028 and BEHAVIOUR-RULES J5.
- 0.9 D69 identical-only duplicate import rows: amended by 0.16 AP6's merging of complementary non-empty values; R032 and BEHAVIOUR-RULES I7.
- 0.10 D29 refusal to rename a key named by a consumer definition: superseded by 0.10 D54's reference rewriting; R035.
- 0.10/early 0.16 AI naming inference: removed in 0.16 AP6a; rules-based inference remains, R038.
- Early 0.16 assistant reading/matching/fixing phases and unpivot turns: superseded by the Claude Code session and AP10/AP13; later AI writing must use current task states.
- Boolean notification-mail choices: superseded by 0.16 AO3's now/digest/off delivery values; J57 and R039.
- 0.13's stopped documentation packages, 0.14's pending API-reference/palette/start-page proposals, 0.15's unbuilt interface translations and 0.17's workbook canvas are not current product promises.

- 0.8 S4 relation-creation-order and sortable-flag restrictions are superseded by S4R1–S4R4; all current non-JSON data columns sort, R066.

- 0.8 sidebar restrictions and one-record tree moves are superseded by 0.9 UA9; touch drag is delivered by 0.11 U1.

- 0.9 C1 identity from a unique attribute is replaced by C2 public record IDs; coalescing and debounce are removed by C17, which uses explicit batching, payload and format.

- 0.9 K5 fixed three-chip and five-expansion limits are replaced by K5b/K6a; R085 records R-F5’s empty replace-cell fix over J6’s earlier blanket no-clear wording.

- 0.10’s all-workspace draft-operation impact is narrowed by 0.11 M3 to workspaces running the draft’s model, R115.

- 0.11 M1 removes the global active-release route, field and filter; releases are assigned per workspace.

- 0.12 Z3’s people/group consumer grants are superseded by A3b’s clients-only rule, R124; D412 makes consumer OpenAPI documents public.

- 0.12 I2’s unconditional refresh-token reuse refusal has R’s limited thirty-second recovery exception, R128; I4’s unproved passkey registration and plain recovery hashes were replaced by proof checks and keyed hashes.

## Reconciliation accounting

C1's earlier extraction accounting is preserved in `_coverage/notes/C1b-RELEASES/PROMISES.before.md`. C1b reconciled the 1,246 retained release leads: 904 merged, 28 superseded, 131 dropped as internal/tooling/test evidence or unbuilt work, and 183 AI/agent leads held for the later wave. Added 110 primary statements and 37 rule entries; eight of those rules were subsequently merged into existing rules. COVERAGE has its own semantic row merges, recorded separately; these are not additional release-lead dispositions.

The original plan extractor collapsed identically numbered decisions from different releases into combined sources. C1b used each lead’s actual statement and the release-scoped decision to resolve that provenance; a shared D-number is not evidence of identical behavior. Resolution JSON files retain the earlier source strings for traceability.

## Reading scope and limits

C1b used the saved C1 notes, plan packets and record extracts as instructed, then reviewed the release record in release-sized units: 0.7, 0.8, 0.9, 0.10, 0.11, 0.12 and 0.16, including their alpha records and delivered-package decisions. The earlier 0.3, 0.5 and 0.6 record was checked for remaining leads. The 0.13 restart/stopped status and the unbuilt 0.14, 0.15 and 0.17 plans add no current release promises; the delivered workspace-model work cited by 0.17 belongs to 0.16. There is no separate 0.3/0.4 implementation plan in the plan family.

This review reuses the earlier reading record and extracts; it is not a claim to have reread every line of every historical plan or every source file. Direct checks covered the release amendments and disputed current statements. Existing code citations remain inherited evidence unless a working note names a fresh check. The final pass checked password bounds, the refresh reuse interval and the change-set rename API/browser distinction. No runtime or browser acceptance tests were run for this documentation-only wave.

The 82 existing non-AI page homes referenced by generic partly judgments were read in full. COVERAGE now distinguishes covered text from named missing clauses; it does not infer coverage from a filename. AI/agent rows remain unchanged, including their older judgments, for the explicitly deferred writing wave. No guide pages or screenshots are added by C1b.

The one writer the routes use gives the same grants, record-type limits (D243), audit actor and created_by. A retry after a lost answer cannot apply twice, as the version it names has moved. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D28 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D29 | Rule: **`origin = {kind: "agent", application, proposedBy}`, a type of its own beside `ProposalOrigin`.** `proposedBy` is the person or client's public ID, and `application` the assistant's name, copied as its row may be removed. `_run` sets it on the session, and `proposed.py` reads it before a task's, so AP-D32 and AP-D33 hold with no second place. Revises AG-D5's `{kind, client}`.. A person's browser edit and their assistant's edit must differ, so the principal cannot decide. AG-D13's "claimed" name stays claimed, so the mark says so. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D29 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D30 | Rule: **`audit_events.channel`, nullable, written `mcp` only,** set in `_name_actors` from the session, for every entry the ORM inserts during a call. Shown as `channel` on `GET /changes` and a record's history, additive. Null means not through an assistant; earlier rows stay null. No screen shows it, as the mark says it for records and relationships.. AG-D16 moved it here. The tools reach only ORM-built events, and one hook covers them. My proposal: no line on the Changes page, because a change-set event's actor already names a client, though for a person it does not say it cam | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D30 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D31 | Rule: **A refused tool call says `CODE: message`, then `details` as JSON,** on every tool, reads included. `_run` records it as now.. The scope, and an assistant needs currentVersion and diagnostics to correct itself. The refusal for a missing and an unreadable workspace stays identical. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D31 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D32 | Rule: **Left out on purpose:** removing a record, relation or value; clearing a field (`value` may not be null); withdrawing; discarding a change set; batches (`bulk` is outside the ceiling); restructuring.. AG-D3's "no deletion of anything", and the exit gate. A row added by mistake is corrected by a person, or the assistant starts a new change set. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D32 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D34 | Rule: **The answer is direct, not a job** (revises AP-D8 for this purpose). `POST /api/v1/workspaces/{key}/query-translations` takes `applicationKey`, `viewKey`, `question` (at most 500 characters), `changeSetId` or `publicationId`, `sendLevel`, and answers `filters`, `search`, `notExpressed` and `applied`. It needs Browse there and Use AI proposals (`ai_requested`). It reads the model, releases its transaction, asks through `calls.ask` and holds none while the provider answers (J48.8: J54.1 reads `pg_stat_activity` from inside the call), then checks the answer in a second transaction. Table views only: another type is `422 QUERY_TRANSLATION_VIEW_UNSUPPORTED`.. One short call with nothing to batch, cancel or resume; the person waits at the box. A job would add a worker round trip and an inbox notice for a second. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D34 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D35 | Rule: **Send level `structure`, and an offer rule of its own** (revised 2026-11-01: names of short lists at `samples` and `values`, see the revision below).** The provider is sent the question, today's date and, per field, its key, label, kind, conditions and, for a relationship, the names of the types it reaches and of the types they can stand under. No record name, value or ID. The UI offers Ask when a provider is set and the person holds ai-proposals in the workspace, at any send level (`queryTranslationOffer`, beside `aiOffer`); hidden, never disabled. It is not drawn on mapping grids, trees or Changes, which are not table views.. The question and a list's field names are the least anything can send, so every level allows it. The user's rule: hide what cannot be done. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D35 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D36 | Rule: **A record named in words is found by the engine, in the database, in the context asked about.** The model gives a name; the engine looks among the proper types (those the relationship reaches for "is any of", those its records can stand under for under and notUnder): the exact name, ignoring case, first; otherwise the one record whose name contains it. None, or several, is a part in `notExpressed` with the reason (up to five names), never a guess. A record created in a change set is found there and not live.. The model sees no record, so it cannot choose one. A wrong guess would change a list silently. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D36 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D37 | Rule: **Nothing is applied when any part is not expressed.** `applied` is false, the filters that could be built are still returned, and the client applies nothing: it shows the part, the proposed filters as dashed pills that do nothing and Use these filters. A sort is a part not expressed (the list's sort is a control of its own); so is a question with nothing to filter by.. The user's rule that a question the list cannot express is said so, not approximated. The person decides whether the rest is wanted. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D37 | source-review |
| PL-AG-D38 | Rule: **(Revised 2026-11-01: the fields are also every relationship of the type.) A field or condition the list does not offer, or a value its validation refuses, makes the whole answer a failed call.** The fields are the view's `filterFields` and, live, the two stamps. Each filter then goes through the list's own validation by building the list's statement with it; a refusal marks the call recorded as answered as `failed` (kind `malformed`), and the route answers `502 QUERY_TRANSLATION_FAILED` with `details.callId`, as it does for a provider that fails. Nothing comes back to apply, and the box keeps its words.. The model's answer is data to check, never a guess to repair (AP1). The UI says one sentence (errorMessage) and search still works. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AG-D38 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D10 | Rule: **Two direct routes, no job.** `POST /api/v1/model-releases/{id}/impact-summary` and `POST /api/v1/model-drafts/{id}/impact-summary`, with `workspaceKey`, optional `sendLevel` and `language`; the release's route compares with the release the workspace runs, the draft's route also takes `expectedVersion`. An invalid draft is `422 IMPACT_SUMMARY_DRAFT_INVALID`. No transaction is held while the provider answers.. One short call with nothing to resume, as AG-D34. Revises AP-D8 for this purpose. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D10 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D11 | Rule: **The answer always carries every fact.** `facts: [{id, kind, text, path, count}]`, `statements: [{text, factIds}]`, `removedStatements`, `failure`, `cached`, `callId`. A provider that fails or answers out of shape is a `200` with `statements` empty and `failure` set, not a `502`.. AO-D9 asks for "facts alone" on a failure, so the route still has something true to give. Differs from AG4, where a failed call had nothing to show. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D11 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D12 | Rule: **A fact's text and ID come from the engine.** Kinds: `change` (one per diff entry), `data` (a compatibility diagnostic with its count), `contract` (a contract diagnostic), `blocked` (a diagnostic that refuses the activation) and `records` (records per touched type). The ID derives from kind, code and path, so the same fact keeps its ID. The text is built from the model's labels.. The reader checks the sentence against a line the AI did not write. count per touched type is one new GROUP BY over the workspace, whole set (AO-D3). / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D12 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D13 | Rule: **"Mapping changes" are relationship and view facts.** No separate kind, and saved model mappings stay out.. That is what a release can change, as found above. The sentence in the journey still reads as written, since a relationship's fact names its types. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D13 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D14 | Rule: **The provider gets at most 40 facts per kind, each with its true count; the person gets all.** The largest counts and blocked items come first; the rest is "and N more" in the context, and a statement may cite the group.. AO-D3. A release of another model can hold hundreds of changes. Text in before and after is cut at 200 characters in the context and sent as it stands in the model. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D14 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D15 | Rule: **The cache is a small table.** `ai_summaries`: `purpose`, `input_hash` (unique), `answer` JSON, `call_id`, `expires_at`, one hour on. The hash covers the facts, language, send level, provider, model and prompt version. A hit makes no call and returns `cached: true`. Expired rows are removed in one statement when a row is written. Migration: one table.. AO-D4's "same facts, same text, no second call" must hold across worker processes, which an in-process cache cannot. One hour is the preview's session in AO-D4. AO2 and AO3 reuse it. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D15 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D16 | Rule: **No new permission group.** AO-D5's "second action" would not be separately grantable, as grants are by group, so a holder of `model.activate` in the workspace who also holds Use AI proposals may ask. The group's description gains "and summaries". Hidden otherwise.. Grants are by group (ADR-044), so a second action changes nothing a person can see. The roles that hold Model release already hold the group. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D16 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D17 | Rule: **One component, only on request.** `ImpactSummary` in `features/model-admin`, in `ActivateDialog` and `ApplyDialog`, after the compatibility notice. Nothing is sent until the person presses **Explain this change**; the line beside it says what is sent. A new check clears the account.. A dialog opening must not spend money or send anything. One component, no copies. Facts appear only after asking, so with no provider the dialogs are as they are today (AP-D3: hidden). / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D17 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D18 | Rule: **The prompt says what the facts are and never judges.** It asks for short statements in the given language, each citing fact IDs, and forbids "safe", "risky" and advice. A check drops a statement with no valid ID (AO-D2). It does not scan words: the wording rule rests on the prompt, the citations and the note under the account.. Filtering words would give false comfort in any language. "Written by AI … It can be wrong" stays under the account. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D18 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D19 | Rule: **`language` is optional in the API and the UI sends none until 0.15 is built.** The default is English. A German account beside English facts is left for when 0.15 converts these dialogs.. Changes AO-D8's route to it, not its aim: an integrator can already ask for a language. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D19 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D21 | Rule: **Counts are of live data.** A record, relationship or field count reads only entries with a feed position. Work in an open change set appears only as that set's events (created, submitted, sent back, approved, discarded). The facts' heading says "live data".. Otherwise a published record counts twice (found above). Cost: edits still sitting in an open change set are not counted, only that the set exists and what happened to it. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D21 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D22 | Rule: **A publication says who prepared it.** A `source` fact for a publication reads its change set's staged entries in one grouped statement: the jobs and the people that prepared it, beside who published. `people` counts direct live changes and that preparation, as "changes".. The journey's "by 3 people, 190 in one bulk import" could otherwise almost never be said, as most bulk work reaches live data as a publication. Cost: one more grouped read over the workspace's entries, timed in the basics. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D22 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D23 | Rule: **Names go out only at `values`.** At `structure` the provider gets counts and the model's labels, and no name of a record, person, change set or file. The reader's fact lines always show them, as Activity does.. Those names are free text and personal data. Narrows AO-D1, which lists "actor" among what is sent. Cost: at structure an account says "one change set was sent back" and the citation names it. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D23 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D24 | Rule: **Two direct routes, no job.** `POST /api/v1/workspaces/{key}/history/account` and `POST /api/v1/objects/{objectTypeKey}/{id}/history/account`, with `since`, `until` (included, not included, as `/changes`), optional `timeZone` (for "on Tuesday"; UTC by default), `sendLevel`, `language`. No period limit. `422 HISTORY_PERIOD_INVALID` for an empty or reversed period or an unknown time zone. Needs Browse and Use AI proposals in the workspace; hidden otherwise.. Revises AO-D7's "job when the period is large", on the measure above, and as AO-D10. If the basics show otherwise it goes in the record, with the figures. A record's account reads through audit_event_subjects. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D24 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D27 | Rule: **Citation to fact, fact to the changes.** A fact carries a `link`: a record, a change set, a publication, a job, or a narrowing of the list. **Show them** narrows Activity in place under `ScopeBanner`, or filters a record's timeline by kind and person. `GET /changes` gains `objectTypeKey`, `causeId` and `live=true`, whole set, additive. A removed job has no link and says "a job, since removed".. The journey asks for links to the records. live is needed so the narrowed list is what the fact counted. Cost: one more filter set, on the JSON subject, within the workspace's rows. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D27 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D28 | Rule: **The facts through the AG2 tools, not the account:** a `history_facts` tool returns the engine's facts for a workspace or a record over a period, with no provider call, and needs Browse only. *(Revised 2026-11-02, approved by the user: the first wording, a `history_account` tool, could never be offered, as an assistant's token is cut to Browse and Work in change sets, AG-D15.)*. AO-D7. The assistant words the facts itself; the agent ceiling is unchanged. Cost: one tool, and no provider account through an assistant. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D28 | source-review |
| PL-AO-D29 | Rule: **Only on request.** The section sits above the Activity filters and on the record's History panel. Nothing is sent until **Explain this period**; the line beside it says what goes, at the level that applies. Default period: 7 days for a workspace, 30 for a record. An empty period makes no call and says so.. As AO-D17. Cost: without a provider the facts are not shown either (AP-D3), as in AO1. / | steward | PL §0.16 plan AO-D29 | source-review |
